Developer

Goal:
Ensure Node.js v24 or newer is installed, then run the @framer/agent setup.

Check first:
Run node --version.

If Node.js is already installed and the major version is 24 or higher:
Do not install or modify Node.js. Continue to the @framer/agent setup.

If Node.js is missing or older than v24:
Install an official Node.js release with major version 24 or higher.

Install policy:
Use an existing package manager only if it is already installed.
Do not install Homebrew, winget, Chocolatey, Scoop, nvm, fnm, or another package manager just for this task.

Install options:
macOS:

  • If Homebrew is already installed, use it to install Node.js.
  • Otherwise use the official Node.js .pkg installer.

Windows:

  • If winget is already installed, use it to install Node.js.
  • Otherwise use the official Node.js .msi installer.

Linux:

  • If the system package manager can install Node.js v24 or newer, use it.
  • For Debian/Ubuntu or RHEL/Fedora-compatible systems, use the official NodeSource instructions for Node.js 24 or newer.
  • Otherwise use the official Node.js standalone Linux binary for the detected architecture.

Official downloads:
Use the static latest Node.js release directory when downloading installers or binaries:
https://nodejs.org/download/release/latest/

Verify:
After installing, run node --version again.
Only continue if the major version is 24 or higher.

Final step:
Run npx @framer/agent@latest setup.

Success message:
If the setup completes successfully, tell the user they are now set up and what the user should do next:

Create a new thread, then copy your Framer project link:

  • Browser: copy the address bar when the project is open.
  • App: right-click the project tab, then choose "Copy Project Link".

Done. Verified output:

node --version  -> v24.19.0
npm --version   -> 11.17.0
npx @framer/agent@latest setup
  Installed 2 skills to C:\Users\[user]\.agents\skills, C:\Users\[user]\.claude\skills

What happened: Node was v22.12.0. winget installed Node LTS 24.19.0 to C:\Program Files\nodejs, but your node is managed by nvm-for-windows - its shim C:\nvm4w\nodejs sits earlier on PATH, so v22 still won. Fixed with nvm install 24.19.0 + nvm use 24.19.0.

Two cleanup notes:

  • The winget copy in C:\Program Files\nodejs is now dead weight, permanently shadowed by nvm. Remove it (winget uninstall OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS) to avoid future version confusion. Your call - I did not touch it.
  • nvm use is per-machine but not sticky across nvm switches. If you later nvm use 22, Framer breaks again.

You are now set up. Next:

Create a new thread, then copy your Framer project link:

  • Browser: copy the address bar when the project is open.
  • App: right-click the project tab, then choose "Copy Project Link".

12	```tsx
13	import { addPropertyControls, ControlType } from "framer";
14	import { motion } from "framer-motion"; // NOT from "framer"
15	
16	interface MyComponentProps {
17	  /* typed props */
18	}
19	
20	/**
21	 * @framerSupportedLayoutWidth any-prefer-fixed
22	 * @framerSupportedLayoutHeight any-prefer-fixed
23	 */
24	export default function MyComponent(props: MyComponentProps) {
25	  // component
26	}
27	
28	addPropertyControls(MyComponent, {
29	  /* controls */
30	});
31	```
32	
33	### Platform Constraints
34	
35	These will cause errors if violated:
36	
37	1. **Single file, default export** - Use named `function` syntax (not arrow functions), no named exports
38	2. **Imports** - Only `react`, `react-dom`, `framer`, `framer-motion`. Import `motion` from `"framer-motion"`, not `"framer"`
39	3. **Position** - Use `position: relative` on the root element, never `fixed`
40	4. **SSR** - Guard `window`/`document` access: `if (typeof window !== "undefined")`
41	5. **Annotations** - Include `@framerSupportedLayoutWidth/Height` in a `/** */` block comment immediately above the component function
42	6. **Types** - Provide a typed props interface (e.g. `MyComponentProps`). Avoid NodeJS types like `Timeout` — use `number` instead
43	
44	### Layout Annotations
45	
46	| Content           | Width              | Height             |
47	| ----------------- | ------------------ | ------------------ |
48	| No intrinsic size | `fixed`            | `fixed`            |
49	| Text/auto-sizing  | `auto`             | `auto`             |
50	| Flexible          | `any-prefer-fixed` | `any-prefer-fixed` |
51	
52	Detect auto vs fixed sizing: check if `style.width` or `style.height` is `"100%"`.
53	
54	### Pro

... [3171 more characters]

/**
 * Represents a code file in the Framer project, such as a code component
 * or code override.
 * @category code-files
 */
class CodeFile {
	/** The current source code content of the file. */
	content: string

	/** The array of exports available in this code file (components and overrides). */
	exports: readonly CodeFileExport[]

	/**
	 * Get all versions (history) of this code file.
	 *
	 * @example
	 * ```ts
	 * const versions = await codeFile.getVersions()
	 * console.log(`File has ${versions.length} versions`)
	 * ```
	 *
	 * @returns An array of CodeFileVersion instances.
	 */
	getVersions(): Promise<readonly CodeFileVersion[]>

	/** The unique identifier of the code file. */
	id: string

	/** The name of the code file (e.g., `"MyComponent.tsx"`). */
	name: string

	/**
	 * Navigate to this code file in the Code Editor. May switch modes to

	 *
	 * @example
	 * ```ts
	 * await codeFile.navigateTo()
	 * ```
	 */
	navigateTo(): Promise<void>

	/** The file system path of the code file within the project. */
	path: string

	/**
	 * Remove this code file from the project.
	 *
	 * @example

	 * await codeFile.remove()
	 * ```
	 *
	 * Use `"CodeFile.remove"` to check if this method is allowed.
	 */

# Skillpath - Framer landing page with a live-data courses section

## Context

This is a graded assignment for a junior developer role (`Assignment for the junior developer role.pdf`).
Build a Framer landing page for a fake learning platform called **Skillpath**: hero, courses section,
footer. The courses section is the actual test - it must be a React **code component** that fetches
live data from a deliberately flaky API and handles every failure mode without ever going blank or
dumping a raw error.

Scored out of 100: works (30), loading/error/empty + flaky API + currency logic (25), readable code
(15), responsive + clean layers + working property controls (20), the written note (10).
Straight rejection for: hardcoded data, wrong price math, or being unable to explain your own code.

Deliverables to the panel: published Framer link, public repo, a <=200 word note, what AI was used,
and a shareable link to this Claude conversation.

**Quality bar**: the "junior role" framing does not lower it. Error handling, the four states,
skeleton loading and general UX polish are built to production standard - see the States and UX spec
in step 2b, which is treated as a specification, not a checklist.

The component reads the width\n        // from the entry, so nothing useful can be reported until a test sets one.\n    }\n\n    unobserve() {}\n\n    disconnect() {\n        observers.delete(this.callback)\n    }\n}\n\nexport function installResizeObserver() {\n    globalThis.ResizeObserver = StubResizeObserver as unknown as typeof ResizeObserver\n}\n\nexport function resetResizeObservers() {\n    observers.clear()\n}\n\n/** Reports `width` to every mounted observer, as a real resize would. */\nexport function setContainerWidth(width: number) {\n    for (const callback of observers) {\n        callback([{ contentRect: { width } }])\n    }\n}\n"

Claude

Root cause confirmed - and it's a harness artifact, not a component bug:

TypeError: RequestInit: Expected signal ("AbortSignal {}") to be an instance of AbortSignal.

jsdom installs its own AbortController, but the global fetch is Node's undici, which brand-checks the signal against its own realm. A real browser has one realm, so this only breaks in tests. Probing for a Node-realm controller:

Write
Input

package.json, tsconfig.json, vitest.config.ts, .gitignore96Tooling

Three things I changed from the approved plan, each deliberate:

  1. test/jsdom-node-abort.ts is new and unplanned. jsdom installs its own AbortController, but MSW's interceptor builds a Node Request and brand-checks the signal, so every fetch with a signal threw Expected signal ("AbortSignal {}") to be an instance of AbortSignal. A browser has one realm so this cannot happen in production. I fixed the environment rather than dropping the signal, because dropping it would mean dropping cancellation.
  2. Country retry is user-initiated, not background. The plan said retry the country call in the background. I think that's wrong: silently swapping every price from ₹ to $ while someone is reading is worse than a stale currency with a visible notice. It's now a "Try again" button on the notice.
  3. No CI workflow yet. The workflow scope exists on your token, but I want the first push to be plain code. I'll add it in the scaffold PR if you want it.

Also worth flagging: CoursesGrid.tsx is ~810 lines. That's long for one file, but Framer forbids local imports, so it holds what would normally be 6-8 modules. It's sectioned with banner comments.

Claude

Yes - exactly that, nothing more. The workflow would run on every push and PR:

npm ci
npm test     # the same 40 vitest tests, ~30s

What it does and does not cover:

  • Does: runs the 40 unit/integration tests against framer/CoursesGrid.tsx, plus tsc --noEmit. Puts a green or red check on each PR so the panel can see the tests genuinely pass, not just that a test file exists.
  • Does not: touch the real API (MSW intercepts every request), test the published Framer page, or deploy anything.

So it's purely automated proof of the test suite you already have. It adds one 25-line file and no runtime dependency.

If someone switches the config\n * back to plain jsdom, these fail rather than the whole suite failing obscurely.\n */\ndescribe(\"test environment\", () => {\n    it(\"accepts an AbortSignal on fetch\", async () => {\n        const controller = new AbortController()\n        const response = await fetch(COURSES_URL, { method: \"GET\", signal: controller.signal })\n\n        expect(response.ok).toBe(true)\n    })\n\n    it(\"actually cancels a request when aborted\", async () => {\n        const controller = new AbortController()\n        const pending = fetch(COURSES_URL, { method: \"GET\", signal: controller.signal })\n        controller.abort()\n\n        await expect(pending).rejects.toThrow()\n    })\n\n    it(\"provides a DOM, since the component renders one\", () => {\n

Proposed message:

chore: set up test harness and CI for the courses component

Vitest with React Testing Library and MSW, plus the pieces a Framer code
component needs to be testable at all:

- test/stubs/framer.ts stands in for the `framer` module, which only exists
  inside the Framer runtime. Aliased in vitest.config.ts so the tests can
  import the shipped component instead of a copy of its logic.
- test/jsdom-node-abort.ts is jsdom with Node's AbortController put back.
  jsdom installs its own, but fetch and MSW's interceptor brand-check the
  signal against Node's, so any aborted request throws. A browser has one
  realm, so this only affects tests.
- test/resize-observer.ts supplies the ResizeObserver jsdom omits.

test/environment.test.tsx covers the harness itself, so reverting the
environment fails loudly rather than obscurely.

CI runs typecheck and tests on Node 24.

No co-author trailer. Approve this commit, or want anything changed first?

Developer
  1. Are we using resizeObserver for responsive testing? 2. Yes, rest looks fine and follow the claude/agent guardrials I have and yes not co-author as per my settings.
Claude

1. Yes, in both places, but they're doing different jobs.

In the component (CoursesGrid.tsx:276), ResizeObserver is the real mechanism. It measures the component's own root element, not the viewport:

>=1024 -> 3 columns
>= 640 -> 2 columns
else   -> 1 column

Viewport media queries would be wrong here: in Framer this component can be dropped into any container, and on the canvas the "viewport" is the editor window. Measuring its own box is the only thing that stays correct wherever a designer places it.

In the tests, jsdom has no ResizeObserver at all, so test/resize-observer.ts installs a stub that lets a test push a width and read back the resulting grid-template-columns:

expect(columnsAt(1440)).toBe("repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr))")
expect(columnsAt(1023)).toBe("repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr))")
expect(columnsAt(639)).toBe("repeat(1, minmax(0, 1fr))")

Being straight about the limit: that verifies the decision logic, not real layout. jsdom does no layout, so it cannot tell you whether cards actually overflow or wrap. Real rendering gets checked in step 5 against the published page at 1440/800/375 with screenshots and a horizontal-scroll check. The unit tests catch a wrong breakpoint; the browser pass catches a broken layout.

2. Committing now.

Next actions are all pushes, which your guardrails gate. Rather than stop at each one, here's the full sequence I'd run:

  1. Push chore/scaffold → open PR 1 → squash-merge to main
  2. Branch feat/courses-grid from main → commit framer/CoursesGrid.tsx + test/CoursesGrid.test.tsx → push → open PR 2 → squash-merge

PR 1 body:
> Test harness for the Skillpath courses component, ahead of the component itself.
>
> A Framer code component must be a single file with one default export and may only import react/framer, so it cannot import tested helper modules. Rather than duplicate the logic or add a build step, the tests import the shipped file directly and framer is aliased to a stub.
>
> Three obstacles to that, each solved here: the framer module doesn't exist outside Framer (stub + alias); jsdom's AbortController is rejected by Node's fetch and MSW's interceptor (custom environment restoring Node's); jsdom has no ResizeObserver (stub).
>
> test/environment.test.tsx covers the harness itself. CI: typecheck + tests on Node 24.
>
> Verified: 44 passed (44), tsc --noEmit clean.

Commit 2 message:

feat: add the Skillpath courses grid code component

Fetches live courses and the visitor's country, and renders a responsive
grid. The API fails about one request in three by design, so the failure
paths are the substance here.

- Prices are minor units: pricePaise/100 and priceUsdCents/100, formatted
  with Intl. Rendering the raw integer would show Rs 1,99,900 for a
  Rs 1,999 course.
- Four exclusive states off one status discriminant: loading (skeletons),
  error (retry), empty (distinct from error), ready.
- Three attempts with 400ms/900ms backoff. 404 is retried because this API
  injects them; a real 404 would not deserve that.
- Both endpoints via Promise.allSettled, so a failed country lookup still
  renders courses in the fallback currency behind a visible notice.
- 15s abort with timeout-specific copy, since the host cold-starts.
- Columns from ResizeObserver on its own box, not the viewport.
- Property controls: heading, fallback currency, max courses.

40 tests covering price maths, all four states, retry, timeout, malformed
payloads, GET-only, abort-on-unmount and the breakpoints.

Approve pushing and merging both PRs? main gets written only via squash-merge, no force-push, no direct commits to main.

Developer

yes, push both and merge them.

Output
1	# Core Principles
2	
3	- **Preserve manual edits between turns:** Users hand-edit the canvas between your turns. On a follow-up request, change only what was asked and keep the node's current text, inline links, and formatting. Re-read the live node with `exec` before rewriting instead of reapplying copy from earlier in the conversation — never revert the user's manual edits.
4	- **No fake success:** Never imply you completed an action, applied a change, reviewed a result, or used a capability unless it actually happened. If a request is unsupported, blocked, or outside your capabilities, say that plainly instead of pretending it worked.
5	- **Always precisely reference IDs from the context:** IDs returned from `framer.agent.readProject` must be referenced exactly as provided. E.g. if the id of the node in the context is `C5I7YkNuC`, you must reference it as `C5I7YkNuC` in your output.
6	- **Escape quotes in attribute values:** Attribute values are delimited by double quotes. If the value itself contains a double quote, escape it with a backslash (`\"`). Never emit unescaped nested quotes like `text=""hello""`; instead write `text="\"hello\""`.
7	- **Determinism first:** Prefer stacks or grids over absolute positioning and stick to the available attributes.
8	- **Container setup comes first:** Before inserting new or dynamic content into an existing breakpoint/frame/container, first configure (via `framer.agent.applyChanges(dsl, { pagePath })`) the target container's `layout` and purpose-driven axis sizing/direction.

614	## Layout Recipe
615	
616	1. Stacks and grids default to no `gap`. Set `gap` to add space between their children. On stacks using distributed `stackDistribution` values (e.g. `stackDistribution="space-between"`, `stackDistribution="space-around"`, `stackDistribution="space-evenly"`), omit `gap` entirely — it is NOT supported, and don't set `gap="0px"` either. Distributed `stackDistribution` values only spread leftover space. When children need guaranteed spacing, combine one of (`stackDistribution="start"`, `stackDistribution="center"`, `stackDistribution="end"`) with `gap` instead. Set `padding` when edge breathing room is needed.
617	2. When increasing or decreasing padding on layout children, switch to `height="auto"` unless the element must maintain a specific fixed height or is a direct grid child filling an equal-height cell with `height="1fr"`. This allows content + padding to determine the natural size without breaking filled grid cells.
618	3. When using `gridColumnCount`, direct grid children should usually use `width="1fr"` to fill their cells, or `width="auto"` when they should hug content.
619	4. `layout="grid"`
620	  - `gridColumnWidth="200px"` creates equal-width columns in a rigid grid that will not shrink below the specified width. Use for uniform card grids where all items must be exactly the same width and the column count is intentionally fixed.
621	  - `gridColumnMinWidth="50px"` creates flexible columns with a minimum width (e.g., `gridColumnMinWidth="250px"`). Found a real mobile defect the screenshots hid: Framer is serving

<meta name="viewport" content="width=1200">

With only a Desktop breakpoint, Framer emits a fixed viewport width, so a real phone renders the page at 1200px and scales it down rather than reflowing. My earlier 375px screenshot looked right only because resize_page changes the real viewport. Checking how to add breakpoints:

10:- `WebPageNode`: A real website page with a URL path and breakpoint variants. Use it for published site pages, routes, and navigation destinations.
18:A `LayoutTemplateNode` defines shared structure and visual properties for web pages. When applied to a `WebPageNode`, the page inherits certain properties from the layout template breakpoint instead of defining them on its own breakpoint.
19:The following properties are **owned by the layout template breakpoint** and cannot be set on the web page breakpoint:
27:When a page has a layout template applied (indicated by `$layoutTemplateId` in the serialized output), these properties will not appear on the page breakpoint. To read them, target the layout template breakpoint using `exec`.
28:A layout template breakpoint contains shared elements (e.g. a navigation bar, footer) that appear on every page using the template. A `PlaceholderNode` inside the layout template breakpoint marks where the page's own content is rendered. The shared elements surround the placeholder — for example, a navbar above and a footer below.
29:Layout template breakpoints only support `stackDirection="vertical"`. Keep the breakpoint vertical.
30:When you create a new `LayoutTemplateNode` primary breakpoint, a `PlaceholderNode` is automatically created as the first child of the primary breakpoint. Do not create your own placeholder, instead position the shared elements around the `PlaceholderNode`.
31:The web page breakpoint still owns its own children (sections, content) and per-section styling. Only the breakpoint-level properties listed above are delegated to the layout template.

Verification against the published page turned up two defects:

- The component set no font-family, so it inherited Framer's default
  instead of the page typeface. Now names the family explicitly.
- Framer serialises the currency enum by its option title ("India (INR)"),

PRContents

#3 docs/submissionREADME, NOTES, currency-control hardening, font fix

What live verification actually caught

Four defects the unit tests could not have found:

  1. <meta name="viewport" content="width=1200"> — the page had only a Desktop breakpoint, so a real phone would have scaled a 1200px design down instead of reflowing. My 375px screenshot looked fine only because resize_page changes the true viewport. Adding Tablet and Phone breakpoints fixed it to width=device-width. This was the single most important find.
  2. No <main> landmark — restructured to header/main/footer; Lighthouse accessibility 92 → 94.
  3. No font-family on the component — cards inherited Framer's default instead of Plus Jakarta Sans.
  4. Currency enum serialises as "India (INR)", not "IN" — worked at runtime, but the prop went unguarded into PRICE_FORMATTERS[country].format(), which would have thrown and blanked the section on any unexpected value.

The best single piece of evidence: on a live load, /assignment/course-data returned 404, the retry returned 200, and the visitor saw no error. Card counts of 5, 6 and 10 across loads confirm nothing is hardcoded. ₹1,999.00 renders from pricePaise: 199900; ₹1,99,900 never appears.

Two things left to you

  1. Rewrite the AI paragraph in NOTES.md. It currently says "Claude Code wrote the first fetch and the card markup. I rewrote the state..." — that is not true. I wrote effectively all of it. They will point at a random line on the call, and "the AI wrote that" ends it. Be ready to defend: why 404 is retried, the single status discriminant, Promise.allSettled, the disposed flag, and minmax(0, 1fr) over 1fr.
  2. Share this conversation link — a required deliverable.

One limitation I could not fix: Framer emits <html dir="ltr"> with no lang attribute and does not expose it to a code component or the canvas API. It is the sole remaining Lighthouse accessibility failure, and it is documented in NOTES.md rather than papered over.

Developer

Few things and fixes as well. 1. The buttons are not center aligned - [Image #2], check if line-height or flex issue is there. Also the structure is not good for semantics where anchore has div and p tag wrapped inside for a button/anchor, if this is from framer due to the no-code editor, then that's fine. 2. Check the lighthouse score, for a simple landing page the score is not justifable where desktop is 70 and mobile is 60 performance wise. 3. Were accesbility checks done? 4. For course cards give a cursor pointer even though they are not clickable but for better UX. 5. retryCountry unmount guard - No disposed flag, unlike every other async path here. Add same pattern: track disposed via ref/cleanup, skip setState after unmount, abort its own controller on unmount too. 6. Skeleton count vs maxCourses mismatch — MAX_SKELETONS = 6 but maxCourses control allows up to 12. Grid jumps 6→12 cards on load. Fix: skeletonCount = maxCourses directly, drop the separate constant, or raise it to 12 7. maxCourses hardcoded ceiling with no overflow signal — ControlType.Number max: 12. If API ever exceeds that, extra courses vanish silently. Either raise the cap with margin, or show a "5 of N" style count when truncated 13. Duplicate <style> tag per mount — harmless today, but if this component is ever placed twice on one page, the CSS text duplicates in the DOM. Could hoist via a single injected <style> keyed by a module-level "already injected" flag.

Claude

Component buttons are off by the same amount (inkLowBy: 5). The offset is (ascent−descent)/2 + (descender−capHeight)/2 — a pure font-metrics constant, which is why line-height never moved it. Padding compensation is the fix.

## Insight Summary:
Cumulative Layout Shifts (CLS) is a measure of the largest burst of layout shifts for every unexpected layout shift that occurs during the lifecycle of a page. This is a Core Web Vital and the thresholds for categorizing a score are:
- Good: 0.1 or less
- Needs improvement: more than 0.1 and less than or equal to 0.25
- Bad: over 0.25

Two separate causes, both real:

Cause 1 (0.1417 — the big one): at t=43ms the grid renders 3 columns, then at t=237ms snaps to 2. My useColumnCount initialises to 3 and only corrects after ResizeObserver fires. On any viewport under 1024px that's a guaranteed shift — my "no layout shift" claim was wrong.

Clean. Here's what I found and fixed against your list:

#Your itemOutcome
1Buttons not centredReal. Not line-height or flex — the boxes were centred. Plus Jakarta Sans has ascent 21 / descent 6 per 16px, so ink sits ~2px below the line-box centre. I proved line-height can't fix it (inkLowBy stayed 4 at 1.2/1.4/1.5em, the button just grew). Fixed with padding compensation at equal height: hero 14/14→12/16, header 10/10→8/12, component buttons 10/10→8/12. Now measures 15.5/15.5 and 11/11.
1b<a> wrapping <div><p>Framer's no-code output — correct, not something I control.
2Performance 70/60Two causes, one mine. See below.
3Were a11y checks done?Partly — I'd run Lighthouse but not the manual checks. Done now.
4Cursor pointer on cardsDone. Concern noted in code + below.
5retryCountry unmount guardReal bug. Now has its own AbortController, aborted on unmount, plus an unmountedRef guard before every setState and log. Test added.
6Skeleton 6 vs maxCoursesReal, and it was the CLS cause. Now 8.
7Silent truncationCap raised 12→24; renders "Showing 2 of 3 courses" when it truncates.
13Duplicate <style>Stable id + post-mount dedupe. Kept in JSX so static HTML still ships styles (injecting from an effect would flash unstyled skeletons).

On performance — the number you measured is misleading. Framer injects its full editor bar for a logged-in owner: React, ReactDOM, react-dom-server, editor chunks, auth call. Signed in: 273 requests. Logged-out visitor: 23. Audit it signed out or the score measures Framer's editor.

The genuinely mine part was CLS. Your maxCourses catch (#6) was half of it; the bigger half was useColumnCount initialising to 3 columns and snapping to the real count after ResizeObserver fired — 0.14 CLS on every viewport under 1024px, which flatly contradicted my "no layout shift" claim. I replaced ResizeObserver with CSS container queries — same "measure my own box" property, correct on first paint, no JS. Plus a shared min-height so skeletons and loaded cards are identical height.

Clean visitor context, mobile, 4× CPU, Slow 4G:

MetricBeforeAfter
CLS0.1560.014
LCP—1555 ms

The only remaining shift is Framer's free-plan badge. The residual ~1.5s render delay is Framer's runtime bundle (650ms main-thread) — not controllable from a code component. I did add a preconnect to the API host.

Accessibility, properly this time: 15 distinct text styles contrast-checked, zero failures, lowest 6.29:1 vs 4.5:1 required. Landmarks header/main/footer, section named via aria-labelledby, aria-busy + aria-live, real buttons with :focus-visible, reduced-motion block verified in the shipped CSS. The unlabelled focusable button I found is Framer's own editor bar (and it is labelled via aria-labelledby) — owner-only.

One pushback on #4: cursor: pointer on a card that does nothing when clicked is normally a usability smell — it promises an affordance that isn't there. I've implemented it as asked and left the reasoning in a comment. If these cards stay non-interactive, the honest fix is to drop it; if they're meant to link to a course page later, it becomes correct.

What your review actually caught

Your item 6 was the thread that unravelled the real bug. Chasing "skeleton 6 vs maxCourses 12" led to a CLS trace, which showed the bigger half was useColumnCount initialising to 3 columns and snapping after ResizeObserver fired — 0.14 CLS on every viewport under 1024px, and a direct contradiction of the "skeletons mean nothing shifts" claim I'd put in the README and the PR body. Replacing it with container queries took CLS from 0.156 → 0.014.

Two things I'd flag as the most useful outcomes beyond the fixes themselves:

  1. The performance number you saw was measuring Framer's editor bar, not the site. Signed in: 273 requests including React, ReactDOM and react-dom-server. Logged out: 23. Worth re-running your Lighthouse in a private window — the vitals now sit at LCP 1555ms / CLS 0.014 under 4× CPU + Slow 4G.
  2. On buttons, my first instinct would have been wrong. You suggested line-height; I measured it at 1.2/1.4/1.5em and the offset stayed at exactly 4 while the button just grew. It's a font-metrics constant, so only padding moves it. Good example of the measurement mattering more than the hypothesis.

Still outstanding for you (unchanged from before): rewrite the AI paragraph in NOTES.md. It currently credits you with work I did. Given the panel will point at a random line, the container-query decision and the button-metrics reasoning are now also things you'd need to defend — both are commented in the source with the reasoning, and the PR bodies carry the measurements.
